\displaystyle=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(x)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}+\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho}(x)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}.

In this setting, based on the techniques given in
[
28
]
, we now prove the following lemma which is a modified version of
[
28
, Proposition 5.1]
.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
u\in L^{p-1}_{sp}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
f\in L^{p-1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfy (
4.1
) along with (
4.2
), and let
1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2
. Then there are families of countable disjoint diagonal balls and off-diagonal cubes,
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\left\{\mathcal{Q}\right\}_{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}}
, respectively, such that

(4.4)

E_{\lambda}\coloneqq\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\subset\left(\bigcup\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{Q}\right)

whenever
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, where

(4.5)

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{c_{d}}{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{c_{d}}{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha+\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right)

for some constant
c_{d}=c_{d}(n,s,p)
independent of
\tau
and
\alpha
. In particular, we have that

(4.6)

\Theta(x_{i},\rho_{x_{i}})\geq\frac{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}{c_{d}}\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta(x_{i},\rho)\leq\frac{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}{c_{d}}\lambda\quad\text{ if }\rho_{x_{i}}\leq\rho\leq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}\,\,(i=1,2,\ldots),

(4.7)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}\leq\frac{c_{oh}}{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}\lambda\quad\text{for any }\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}

and

(4.8)

\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq\frac{c_{od}}{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{\,|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>c_{u}\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

for some constants
c_{oh}=c_{oh}(n,s,p,\tilde{p},\tilde{\gamma})
and
c_{od}=c_{od}(n,s,p,\tilde{p})
independent of
\tau
and
\alpha
, where
c_{u}=\frac{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}{c_{d}}
and

(4.9)

\tilde{\gamma}=\begin{cases}\frac{n\tilde{p}}{n-s\tilde{p}}&\quad\text{if }s\tilde{p}<n,\\
2q&\quad\text{if }s\tilde{p}\geq n.\end{cases}

Proof.

Step 1. Diagonal balls and Vitali’s covering.
We first find diagonal balls satisfying (
4.6
) using the exit time argument and Vitali’s covering lemma.
Let us consider a constant
\lambda
satisfying

(4.10)

\displaystyle\lambda

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\kappa}\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha+\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right),

where a free parameter
\kappa\in(0,1]
will be selected later. We next define

D_{\kappa\lambda}=\left\{(x,x)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,\sup_{0<\rho\leq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}}\Theta(x,\rho)>\kappa\lambda\right\}.

We now note from (
2.4
) that for any
x\in B_{r_{1}}
,

(4.11)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}}\left(x\right)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{2}\right)}{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}}\left(x\right)\right)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

and

(4.12)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}}\left(x\right)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}.

Combine (
4.3
), (
4.10
), (
4.11
) and (
4.12
) to see that

\displaystyle\Theta\left(x,\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}\right)\leq\kappa\lambda\quad\text{for any $x\in B_{r_{1}}$}.

Thus, there is an exit radius
\rho_{x}\in\left(0,\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}\right]
such that

(4.13)

\Theta(x,\rho_{x})\geq\kappa\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad\Theta(x,\rho)\leq\kappa\lambda\quad\text{ if }\rho_{x}\leq\rho\leq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10},

for each
(x,x)\in D_{\kappa\lambda}
.
Using the Vitali covering lemma, we have a family of countable disjoint diagonal balls
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
satisfying

(4.14)

\bigcup_{(x,x)\in D_{\kappa\lambda}}\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x}}\left(x\right)\subset\bigcup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right).

Step2. Dyadic cubes and Calderón-Zygmund decomposition.
In this step, we find a collection of dyadic cubes contained in
\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}
which covers
\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}
and apply Calderón-Zygmund decomposition to the each dyadic cube in order to find a covering of
E_{\lambda}
. We first find finitely many disjoint dyadic cubes
\{\mathcal{K}_{j}\}_{j}
such that

\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\subset\bigcup_{j}\{\mathcal{K}_{j}\}\subset\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{K}_{j}\cap\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\neq\emptyset.

This follows from
[
28
, Proposition 5-5B]
. In particular, the cube is of the form
\mathcal{K}_{j}=\mathcal{Q}_{2^{-k_{0}}}\left(2^{-k_{0}}X_{j}\right)
for some
X_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}\times\mathbb{Z}^{n}
and the positive integer
k_{0}
such that

(4.15)

\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{20}\leq 2^{-k_{0}}20\sqrt{n}<\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}.

We recall
P^{d}\mathcal{K}=K^{d}\times K^{d}
for a given cube
\mathcal{K}=K^{1}\times K^{2}
.
In this setting, we note from (
2.4
), (
2.6
) and (
4.10
) that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{K}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}+\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{K}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{B}_{2})}{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{K})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}+\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{B}_{2})}{\mu_{\tau}(P^{d}\mathcal{K})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 3\left(2^{n}\frac{\nu_{0}+1}{\tau}\left(\frac{800\sqrt{n}}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda

for any
\mathcal{K}\in\left\{\mathcal{K}_{j}\right\}
by taking
\kappa\in(0,\kappa_{0}]
, where

(4.17)

\kappa_{0}=\left(\frac{\tau}{(\nu_{0}+1)(80n)^{4n}4^{\tilde{p}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}.

We next observe a simple fact for a dyadic cube
\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}
and its predecessor
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=\tilde{Q}^{1}\times\tilde{Q}^{2}
. If
\mathrm{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)\geq d\left(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\right)
, where we define
d\big({\mathcal{Q}}\big)=\text{side length of }{\mathcal{Q}}
, then

(4.18)

\mathrm{dist}\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)\geq\mathrm{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)\geq d\left(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\right)>d(\mathcal{Q}).

In light of (
4.16
) and (
4.18
), we apply the same arguments as in the proof of the classical Calderón-Zygmund decomposition lemma to each cube
\mathcal{K}\in\left\{\mathcal{K}_{j}\right\}
, in order to find a family of countable disjoint dyadic cubes
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}^{i}\right\}
such that
\mathcal{Q}^{i}\subset\mathcal{K}_{j}
for some
j
satisfying

(4.19)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}^{i}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{A}(\mathcal{Q}^{i})\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q}^{i})}{\dist(Q^{i,1},Q^{i,2})}\right)^{s+\alpha+\tau}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}^{i}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right]>\lambda\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{A}(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{i})\left(\frac{d(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}^{i}})}{\dist(\tilde{Q}^{i,1},\tilde{Q}^{i,2})}\right)^{s+\alpha+\tau}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right]\leq\lambda,

where
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}=\tilde{Q}^{i,1}\times\tilde{Q}^{i,2}
is the predecessor of
\mathcal{Q}^{i}=Q^{i,1}\times Q^{i,2}
and

(4.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}\left(\mathcal{Q}^{i}\right)=\begin{cases}1&\quad\text{if }\mathrm{dist}\left(Q^{i,1},Q^{i,2}\right)\geq d(\mathcal{Q}^{i}),\\
0&\quad\text{if }\mathrm{dist}\left(Q^{i,1},Q^{i,2}\right)<d(\mathcal{Q}^{i}).\end{cases}

In addition, if
(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{Q}^{i}
, then by using Hölder’s inequality and (
4.19
), we find a sequence of dyadic cubes
\mathcal{K}^{j}\notin\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
containing
(x,y)
such that

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{K}^{j}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{K}^{j}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\leq\lambda\quad\text{and}\quad\mathrm{diam}\left(\mathcal{K}^{j}\right)\to 0\text{ as }j\to\infty.

Therefore, the fact that
\mu_{\tau}
has the doubling property (
2.5
) and is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure
\,dx\,dy
yields that

(4.21)

|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|\leq\lambda\quad\mbox{a.e. on $\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{Q}^{i}$ with respect to the measure $\mu_{\tau}$.}

Step 3. First elimination of nearly diagonal cubes.

We first want to reduce the family of off-diagonal cubes as follows:
For any
\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
,

(4.22)

\mathcal{Q}\subset\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)

provided that

(4.23)

\dist\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)<d\left(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\right).

To this end, we note from (
4.19
) and (
4.20
) that one of the following three inequalities must hold:

(4.24)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}>\frac{\lambda}{3},\quad\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{1}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}>\frac{\lambda}{3},

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{2}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}>\frac{\lambda}{3}.

Suppose that the first inequality in (
4.24
) holds.
In light of
[
28
, Proposition 5.3]
, we observe that there are points
(x_{0},x_{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{2n}
and
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=\tilde{Q}^{1}\times\tilde{Q}^{2}
such that

\dist\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)=\sqrt{2}\dist(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}},(x_{0},x_{0}))=\sqrt{2}|(x_{1},x_{2})-(x_{0},x_{0})|.

Then using (
4.23
), we find that for any
(a,b)\in\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
,

\displaystyle|(a,b)-(x_{0},x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq|(a,b)-(x_{1},x_{2})|+|(x_{1},x_{2})-(x_{0},x_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{n}d(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\dist(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq 4\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})+\frac{2}{\sqrt{2}}d\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q}),

which implies that
\mathcal{Q}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\subset\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(x_{0})
. Since
\mathcal{Q}\cap\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\neq\emptyset
, we take
z_{0}\in Q^{1}\cap B_{r_{1}}
. Then we observe that

(4.25)

\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(x_{0})\subset\mathcal{B}_{20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(z_{0}),

where we have used fact that
z_{0}\in Q^{1}\subset B_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(x_{0})\subset B_{20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(z_{0})
.
Note from (
2.6
), the first inequality in (
4.24
) and (
4.17
) that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}(z_{0},20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q}))}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}{\mu_{\tau}\Big(\mathcal{B}\big(z_{0},20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})\big)\Big)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\tau}{2^{n}v_{0}(20\sqrt{n})^{2n}}\left(\frac{\lambda}{3}\right)^{\tilde{p}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\kappa\lambda\right)^{\tilde{p}}.

Using (
4.13
), (
4.26
) and the fact that
z_{0}\in B_{r_{1}}
and
20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})<\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}
by (
4.15
), we have

(4.27)

\mathcal{B}_{20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(z_{0})\subset\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{z_{0}}}\left(z_{0}\right).

From (
4.14
), (
4.25
) and (
4.27
), we have (
4.22
). We next assume that the second inequality in (
4.24
) holds. We write
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{d(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})}\left(x,y\right)
to see that
P^{1}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}=\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{d(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})}\left(x\right)
.
For any
(a,b)\in\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}
, we find that

\displaystyle|(a,b)-(x,x)

\displaystyle|\leq|(a,b)-(x,y_{2})|+|(x,y_{2})-(x,y_{1})|+|(x,y_{1}),(x,x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3}{2}\sqrt{n}d(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})+\mathrm{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{n}d\left(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\right)

\displaystyle<10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q}),

where
y_{1}\in\tilde{Q^{1}}
and
y_{2}\in\tilde{Q}^{2}
such that
|y_{1}-y_{2}|=\mathrm{dist}\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)
. Thus, this yields that
\mathcal{Q}\subset\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\subset\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(x)\subset\mathcal{B}_{20\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(z_{0})
for some
z_{0}\in B_{r_{1}}
. Like in the estimate of (
4.26
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(x)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mu_{\tau}\left(P^{1}\mathcal{Q}\right)}{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})}(x)\right)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{1}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\geq\left(\kappa\lambda\right)^{\tilde{p}},

where we have used (
2.4
) and the second inequality in (
4.24
) for the third inequality. This implies (
4.22
).
Similarly, if the third inequality in (
4.24
) is true, then we have (
4.22
). Therefore, we now restrict our concentration on a subfamily of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
,
\mathcal{A}
which is defined by

(4.28)

\mathcal{A}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\,\;\,\mathcal{Q}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\setminus\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x\right)\right\}.

Then we note from (
4.22
), (
4.23
) and (
4.18
) that

(4.29)

\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)\geq\dist\left(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2}\right)\geq d\left(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\right)>d(\mathcal{Q})\quad\mbox{for any $\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\in\mathcal{A}$.}

Step 4. Off-diagonal reverse Hölder’s inequality.

We now introduce an important lemma to handle the off-diagonal cubes in
\mathcal{A}
.

Lemma 4.2

(Reverse Hölder inequality on off-diagonal cubes)
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}
be a cube such that

(4.30)

\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)\geq d(\mathcal{Q}).

Then there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p,\tilde{p},\tilde{\gamma})
independent of
\tau
and
\alpha
such that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{s+\alpha+\tau}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right].

Proof.

We note from (
4.30
) that for any
x\in Q^{1}
and
y\in Q^{2}
,

|x-y|\leq|x-x_{0}|+|x_{0}-y_{0}|+|y_{0}-y|\leq\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})+\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})+\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})\leq 3\sqrt{n}\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2}),

where
x_{0}\in Q^{1}
and
y_{0}\in Q^{2}
such that
\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})=|x_{0}-y_{0}|
.
Therefore, we have that

(4.31)

\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)\leq|x-y|\leq 3\sqrt{n}\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)

whenever
x\in Q^{1}
and
y\in Q^{2}
. The above inequality (
4.31
) and Jensen’s inequality yield that

(4.32)

\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n}}{c(n)\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)^{n-p\tau}}\leq\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n}}{\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)^{n-p\tau}}

and

\displaystyle\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)^{n-p\tau}}{d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n}}\frac{1}{\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)^{n-p\tau+\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}}\int_{Q^{1}}\int_{Q^{2}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{2}\frac{c\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)^{-\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}}{d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n}}\int_{Q^{1}}\int_{Q^{2}}|u(x)-(u)_{Q^{i}}|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,dx\,dy

\displaystyle+\frac{c\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)^{-\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}}{d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n}}\int_{Q^{1}}\int_{Q^{2}}|(u)_{Q^{1}}-(u)_{Q^{2}}|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,dx\,dy\eqqcolon\sum_{i=1}^{2}I_{i}+J

for some constant
c=c(n,\tilde{\gamma})
. We now further estimate
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
and
J
.

Estimates of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
.

From Lemma
2.2
together with (
4.9
), there is a constant
c=c(n,s,\tilde{p},\tilde{\gamma})
such that

(4.33)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}}|u-(u)_{Q^{1}}|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}\leq cd\left(Q^{1}\right)^{s}\left(\int_{Q^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{\tilde{p}}}{|x-y|^{n+s\tilde{p}}}\,dx\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}.

In light of (
4.33
), the fact that

1\leq\left(\frac{2d(\mathcal{Q})}{|x-y|}\right)^{\tilde{p}(\alpha+\tau)-p\tau}\quad\text{any }x,y\in Q^{1}

and (
2.4
), we estimate
I_{1}
as

\displaystyle I_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})^{-\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}}{d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n}}d(\mathcal{Q})^{2n+\tilde{\gamma}s}\left(\int_{Q^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{\tilde{p}}}{|x-y|^{n+s\tilde{p}}}\,dx\,dy\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{cd(\mathcal{Q})^{\tilde{\gamma}s}}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})^{\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}}\left(\int_{Q^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}}\left(\frac{2d(\mathcal{Q})}{|x-y|}\right)^{\tilde{p}(\alpha+\tau)-p\tau}\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|^{\tilde{p}}}{|x-y|^{n+s\tilde{p}}}\,dx\,dy\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}\times Q^{1}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\tilde{p}}}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p,\tilde{p},\tilde{\gamma})
. Likewise, we have

I_{2}\leq c\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{2}\times Q^{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{\tilde{p}}}.

Estimate of
J
.

In light of Jensen’s inequality, (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), we estimate
J
as

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq c\frac{1}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})^{\tilde{\gamma}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{2}}|u(x)-u(y)|^{p}\,dx\,dy\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{1}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{Q^{2}}\left(\frac{|u(x)-u(y)|}{|x-y|^{s+\alpha+\tau}}\right)^{p}\,dx\,dy\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{\tilde{\gamma}}{p}}.

Combine all the estimates
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
and
J
to get the desired result.
∎

Remark 6
.

We now prove that if
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}
, then there is a constant
c_{oh}=c_{oh}(n,s,p,\tilde{p},\tilde{\gamma})
such that

(4.34)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{\gamma}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{\gamma}}}\leq c_{oh}\lambda\quad\text{for any }\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}.

By
p\leq\tilde{p}
,
\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\subset 4\mathcal{Q}
and
P^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}\subset 4P^{d}\mathcal{Q}
, we observe that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\leq\left(\frac{\mu_{\tau}(4\mathcal{Q})}{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

and

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{s+\alpha+\tau}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{d(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})}{\dist(\tilde{Q}^{1},\tilde{Q}^{2})}\right)^{s+\alpha+\tau}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{\mu_{\tau}(4P^{d}\mathcal{Q})}{\mu_{\tau}(P^{d}\mathcal{Q})}\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\right].

Using (
4.19
), (
4.29
), Lemma
4.2
, (
2.5
) and the above observations, we have (
4.34
).

Step 5. Decomposition of
\mathcal{A}
.

We now decompose the family
\mathcal{A}
into
AD_{\lambda}=\bigcap\limits_{d=1}^{2}AD_{\lambda}^{d}
and
ND_{\lambda}=\bigcup\limits_{d=1}^{2}ND_{\lambda}^{d}
, where we define

(4.35)

AD_{\lambda}^{d}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}\,;\,\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\leq\left(\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)^{\tilde{p}}\right\}

and

(4.36)

ND_{\lambda}^{d}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}\,;\,\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}>\left(\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)^{\tilde{p}}\right\}.

We now estimate
\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})
for each cubes
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}
.

Step 6. Measure estimate of
\mathcal{Q}\in AD_{\lambda}
.

We first estimate
\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})
for
\mathcal{Q}\in AD_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

There is a constant
c_{ad}=c_{ad}(\tilde{p})
such that for every
\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\in AD_{\lambda}
,

(4.37)

\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq\frac{c_{ad}}{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.

Proof.

In light of (
4.19
) and Jensen’s inequality, we have that

(4.38)

\displaystyle\lambda^{\tilde{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 3^{\tilde{p}-1}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle+3^{\tilde{p}-1}\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{p}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\right].

Note that

(4.39)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\leq(\kappa\lambda)^{\tilde{p}}+\frac{1}{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}\int_{\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.

Taking into account (
4.17
), (
4.29
), (
4.35
), (
4.38
) and (
4.39
), we find that

\frac{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}{2}\leq\frac{3^{\tilde{p}-1}}{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}\int_{\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau},

which implies the desired result (
4.37
).
∎

Step 7. Measure estimate of
\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}
.

We next want to estimate
\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})
for
\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}
. To do this, we introduce families of countable diagonal cubes

P^{d}ND_{\lambda}=\left\{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\,;\,\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\in ND_{\lambda}\right\}\quad(d=1,2).

Then we observe the following two facts (see
[
28
, Section 5]
):

(1)

P^{1}ND_{\lambda}=P^{2}ND_{\lambda}
.

(2)

There is a subfamily of disjoint cubes
NH\subset P^{1}ND_{\lambda}
such that

(4.40)

\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{H}\in NH}\mathcal{H}=\bigcup_{\mathcal{Q}\in P^{1}ND_{\lambda}}\mathcal{Q}=\bigcup_{\mathcal{Q}\in P^{2}ND_{\lambda}}\mathcal{Q}.

We now introduce the following lemma which gives an information of qualitative distance between
Q^{1}
and
Q^{2}
for
\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\in ND_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\in ND_{\lambda}
. Assume that
Q^{d}\times Q^{d}\subset\mathcal{H}
for some
d\in\{1,2\}
and
\mathcal{H}\in NH
. Then we have

\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)\geq d(\mathcal{H}).

Proof.

We first assume
d=1
. We prove this Lemma by contradiction. Suppose that

(4.41)

\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)<d(\mathcal{H}).

Since
\mathcal{Q}
and
\mathcal{H}
are dyadic cubes with
d(\mathcal{Q})\leq\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)<d(\mathcal{H})
, we observe

(4.42)

2d(\mathcal{Q})\leq d(\mathcal{H}).

Let
(x_{c},x_{c})
be the center of
\mathcal{H}
. Then we find that for
\mathcal{B}\equiv\mathcal{B}_{\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}}\left(x_{c}\right)
,

(4.43)

\mathcal{B}\subset\mathcal{H}\subset\sqrt{n}\mathcal{B}.

For any
(x,y)\in\mathcal{Q}
, we first note from (
4.41
) and (
4.42
) that

(4.44)

\displaystyle|(x,y)-(x_{c},x_{c})|

\displaystyle\leq|(x,y)-(x,y^{\prime})|+|(x,y^{\prime})-(x_{c},x^{\prime})|+|(x_{c},x^{\prime})-(x_{c},x_{c})|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{Q})+\sqrt{n}\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}+\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)+\sqrt{n}\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{n}\left[\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}+\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}+\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}\right]+\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)<5\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H}),

where
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\in\mathcal{Q}
such that
\dist\left(Q^{1},Q^{2}\right)=|x^{\prime}-y^{\prime}|
.
Thus, (
4.43
) and (
4.44
) imply that
\mathcal{Q},\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{B}_{5\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(x_{c}\right)
. In addition, by following the same arguments as in (
4.25
) and using the fact that
\mathcal{B}_{5\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(x_{c}\right)\subset\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right)
, there is a point
z_{0}\in B_{r_{1}}\cap Q^{1}
such that

(4.45)

\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right).

Then using (
4.36
), (
4.40
), (
4.45
) and (
2.4
), we observe that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\lambda}{4}\right)^{\tilde{p}}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{H}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tau}\frac{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right)\right)}{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\frac{d(\mathcal{H})}{2}}\left(x_{c}\right)\right)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle=(20\sqrt{n})^{n+p\tau}\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau},

which implies that

(4.46)

(\kappa\lambda)^{\tilde{p}}<\frac{\tau}{(20n)^{2n}4^{\tilde{p}}}\lambda^{\tilde{p}}<\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right)}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.

Since (
4.15
) gives that
10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})<\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}
, we find that
\mathcal{B}_{10\sqrt{n}d(\mathcal{H})}\left(z_{0}\right)\subset\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})
, where we have used (
4.13
), (
4.46
) and the fact that
z_{0}\in B_{r_{1}}
. Thus, this contradicts the definition of the family
\mathcal{A}
given in (
4.28
).
We similarly prove the case for
d=2
.
∎

With the above lemma, we now prove the following measure estimates for the cubes in
ND_{\lambda}
.

Lemma 4.5
.

There is a constant
c_{nd}=c_{nd}(n,s,p,\tilde{p})
such that

(4.47)

\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq\frac{c_{nd}}{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{\,|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}
.
We then note from (
4.29
) that

(4.48)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{p}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(\kappa\lambda)^{\tilde{p}}}{\tau}+\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{p}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left[\frac{1}{\tau}\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{\tau}(P^{d}\mathcal{Q})}\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\right]

for
d=1
and 2.
In light of (
4.19
), (
4.17
), Jensen’s inequality, (
4.39
) and (
4.48
), we have that

(4.49)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}{2}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq 3^{\tilde{p}-1}\int_{\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle+3^{\tilde{p}-1}\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{p}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}{\mu_{\tau}(P^{d}\mathcal{Q})}\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\right].

We now denote

(4.50)

\displaystyle K^{d}=\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}^{d}}\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{\tilde{p}\left(s+\alpha+\tau\right)}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}{\mu_{\tau}(P^{d}\mathcal{Q})}\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\right]

for
d=1
and
2
.
To estimate
K^{d}
for each
d=1
and 2, we investigate families of countable disjoint cubes

(4.51)

ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}^{d}\,;\,P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{H}\right\}\quad(d=1,2)

for
\mathcal{H}\in NH
. We note from (
4.40
) that

ND^{d}_{\lambda}=\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{H}\in NH}ND_{\lambda}^{d}(\mathcal{H}),

where
\left\{ND_{\lambda}^{d}(\mathcal{H})\right\}_{\mathcal{H}\in NH}
are mutually disjoint classes.
Using (
4.51
), we first find countable mutually disjoint families of cubes
\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i}
such that

\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\,;\,d(\mathcal{Q})=2^{-i}d(\mathcal{H})\right\}\quad(i\geq 0)

and

ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})=\bigcup\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i}.

In light of Lemma
4.4
, we next decompose

\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i}
into subfamilies
\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j}
which are defined by

\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i}\,;\,2^{j}d(\mathcal{H})\leq\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})<2^{j+1}d(\mathcal{H})\text{ for }\mathcal{Q}=Q^{1}\times Q^{2}\right\}

for
j\geq 0
. To further decompose
\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j}
into the smaller families, we denote all diagonal dyadic cubes in
\mathcal{H}
with the side-length
2^{-i}d(\mathcal{H})
by
\mathcal{H}_{i}(k)=H_{i}(k)\times H_{i}(k)
for
i\geq 0
and
k=1,2,\ldots,2^{in}
.
From the above notation, we set

\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}=\left\{\mathcal{Q}\in\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j}\,;\,P^{d}\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{H}_{i}(k)\right\}.

Then
\left\{\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}\right\}_{i,j,k\geq 0}
is a class of the mutually disjoint families such that

ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})=\bigcup\limits_{i,j,k\geq 0}\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}.

We first note that for any
\mathcal{Q}\in\left[ND^{1}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}
,
\mathcal{Q}
is of the form
H_{i}(k)\times Q^{2}
for some dyadic cube
Q^{2}
satisfying

(4.52)

d(Q^{2})=2^{-i}d(\mathcal{H})\quad\text{and}\quad 2^{j}d(\mathcal{H})\leq\dist(H_{i}(k),Q^{2})<2^{j+1}d(\mathcal{H}).

Thus, we find that there exists a constant
c
depending only on
n
such that

(4.53)

\left|\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}\right|\leq c2^{n(i+j)},

where
\left|\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}\right|
denotes the number of the cubes in
\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}
.
From the decomposition of
ND_{\lambda}^{d}(\mathcal{H})
, we now estimate
K^{d}
for each
d=1
and
2
.
We first note from (
2.4
) and (
4.32
) that there is a constant
c=c(n)
such that

(4.54)

\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})}{\mu_{\tau}(P^{d}\mathcal{Q})}\leq c\frac{\tau}{\nu_{0}}\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{n-p\tau}.

According to (
4.54
), the fact that
p\tau\leq\tilde{p}\tau
and (
4.52
), we find

\displaystyle K^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\nu_{0}}\sum_{\mathcal{H}\in H}\sum_{i,j,k}\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}}\left(\frac{d(\mathcal{Q})}{\dist(Q^{1},Q^{2})}\right)^{n-p\tau+\tilde{p}(s+\alpha)+\tilde{p}\tau}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{\nu_{0}}\sum_{\mathcal{H}\in H}\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\sum_{k=1}^{2^{in}}\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in\left[ND^{d}_{\lambda}(\mathcal{H})\right]_{i,j,k}}\left(2^{-(i+j)}\right)^{n+\tilde{p}(s+\alpha)}

\displaystyle\times\left(\int_{\mathcal{H}_{i}(k)\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right).

Using (
4.53
) and the fact that
\mathcal{H}_{i}(k)
is disjoint for each
i
and
k
, we further estimate
K^{d}
as

\displaystyle K^{d}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\nu_{0}}\sum_{\mathcal{H}\in H}\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\sum_{k=1}^{2^{in}}c\left(2^{-(i+j)}\right)^{\tilde{p}(s+\alpha)}\left(\int_{\mathcal{H}_{i}(k)\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\nu_{0}}\sum_{\mathcal{H}\in H}\sum_{i,j\geq 0}c\left(2^{-(i+j)}\right)^{sp}\left(\int_{\mathcal{H}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\sum_{\mathcal{H}\in H}\left(\int_{\mathcal{H}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p)
,
For the last inequality, we have used the fact that

(4.55)

\sum_{i,j\geq 0}\left(2^{-(i+j)}\right)^{a}\leq\frac{2^{a}}{a\mathrm{ln}2}\quad(a>0).

Since
NH
is the family of disjoint dyadic cubes and
\mathcal{H}\subset\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}
, the above estimate yields that

(4.56)

K^{d}\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.

Then we note from (
4.35
), (
4.38
), (
4.49
), (
4.50
) and (
4.56
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}^{1}\cap AD_{\lambda}^{2}}\frac{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}{4}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})

\displaystyle\leq 3^{\tilde{p}-1}\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}^{1}\cap AD_{\lambda}^{2}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}+3^{\hat{p}-1}K^{1}

\displaystyle\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau},

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p,\tilde{p})
. Likewise, we deduce that there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p,\tilde{p})
such that

\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}^{2}\cap AD_{\lambda}^{1}}\frac{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}{4}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

and

\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in ND_{\lambda}^{1}\cap ND_{\lambda}^{2}}\frac{\lambda^{\tilde{p}}}{4}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq c\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|>\kappa\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.

By combining the above three estimates, we obtain the desired estimate (
4.47
).
∎

Step 8. The choice of constants.
We now take

\kappa=\frac{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}{(\nu_{0}+1)(80n)^{8n}}.

Using the fact that
\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq n\tau^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}
by (
3.13
), we have
\kappa\leq\kappa_{0}
which is given in (
4.17
).
We next choose

c_{d}=\frac{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}{\kappa},\quad c_{u}=\frac{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}{c_{d}}\quad\mbox{and}\quad c_{od}=c_{ad}+c_{nd},

where
c_{d}=c_{d}(n,s,p)
and
c_{od}=c_{od}(n,s,p,\tilde{p})
.

Step 9. Conclusion of the proof.

Let
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
given in (
4.5
). Then
\lambda
satisfies (
4.10
). Therefore, in light of (
4.14
), (
4.21
) and (
4.28
), we find (
4.4
). In addition, taking into account
(
4.34
), (
4.37
) and (
4.47
), we prove (
4.6
), (
4.7
) and (
4.8
). This completes the proof of Lemma
4.1
.
∎

We finish this section by providing estimates which will be needed for the above comparison lemma.

Remark 7
.

For each diagonal ball
\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})
obtained by Lemma
4.1
, we want to show that if

(4.57)

\alpha+\tau<\frac{s}{p-1},

then

(4.58)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq\frac{c}{\frac{s}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau)}\lambda,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq\frac{c}{\frac{s}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau)}\delta\lambda,

where
c=c(n,s,p)
. To this end, we divide the range of
20\rho_{x_{i}}
into

(4.59)

\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{20}<20\rho_{x_{i}}\leq 2(r_{2}-r_{1})

and

(4.60)

0<20\rho_{x_{i}}\leq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{20}.

(1). Assume (
4.59
) is true. Using (
2.4
), Hölder’s inequality and (
4.5
), we get that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\frac{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{2}\right)}{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}\left(\frac{40}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{2n}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n)
. Similarly, we find

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\leq\frac{1}{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}\left(\frac{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{2}\right)}{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}\leq c\delta\lambda,

where we have used the fact that
\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq n\tau^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}
by (
3.13
). On the other hand, in light of (
4.2
) and the fact that

\frac{1}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}}\leq\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\alpha+\tau+s}\leq\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{sp}{p-1}},

we estimate the tail term of
u
as

\displaystyle\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{sp}{p-1}}\Tailp\left(u-(u)_{B_{2}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{sp}{p-1}}\Tailp\left((u)_{B_{2}}-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\eqqcolon\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{sp}{p-1}}T_{1}+\left(\frac{20}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{\frac{sp}{p-1}}T_{2}.

By the fact that
(|a|+|b|)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\leq 2|a|^{\frac{1}{p-1}}+2|b|^{\frac{1}{p-1}}
and
|y-x_{i}|\geq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{2}|y|
for any
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2}
, we further estimate
T_{1}
as

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\left(20\rho_{x_{i}}\right)^{sp}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{2}}|^{p-1}}{|y-x_{i}|^{n+sp}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle+2\left(\left(20\rho_{x_{i}}\right)^{sp}\int_{B_{2}\setminus B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{2}}|^{p-1}}{|y-x_{i}|^{n+sp}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(2^{n+2sp}\left(\frac{1}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus B_{2}}\frac{|u(y)-(u)_{B_{2}}|^{p-1}}{|y|^{n+sp}}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}

\displaystyle+2\left(\left(\frac{40}{r_{2}-r_{1}}\right)^{n}|B_{1}|\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}|u(y)-(u)_{B_{2}}|^{p-1}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}.

Applying Hölder’s inequality and (
2.12
) to the second term in the above last inequality, we observe that there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p)
such that

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{n}{p-1}}}\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{n}{p-1}}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{n}{p-1}}}\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{n}{p-1}}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}.

For the last inequality, we have used Hölder’s inequality. We next estimate
T_{2}
as

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{n}{p-1}}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}|u(y)-(u)_{B_{2}}|^{p-1}\,dy\right)^{\frac{1}{p-1}}\leq\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{\frac{n}{p-1}}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}},

where we have used (
2.2
), (
2.11
), Hölder’s inequality and (
2.12
). Combine all the estimates
T_{1}
and
T_{2}
together with (
4.5
) and the fact that
\frac{sp}{p-1}+\frac{n}{p-1}\leq 2n
to see that

(4.61)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq c\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p)
. Likewise, by following the same lines for the proof of (
4.61
) with
u(x)
,
s
,
\tilde{p}
,
sp
and
\lambda
there, replaced by
f(x)
,
0
,
\tilde{q}
,
s
and
\delta\lambda
, respectively, we have

(4.62)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq c\delta\lambda.

Since
1\leq\frac{1}{\frac{s}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau)}
which follows by (
4.57
), (
4.61
) and (
4.62
) imply (
4.58
).

(2). We now assume (
4.60
). Then there is a positive integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that

(4.63)

\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{20}<2^{k}20\rho_{x_{i}}\leq\frac{r_{2}-r_{1}}{10}.

Applying Lemma
2.6
with
\Omega=B_{2}
,
t=s
,
\rho=20\rho_{x_{i}}
,
x_{0}=x_{i}
and
i=k
, we have that

\displaystyle\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c2^{-k\frac{sp}{p-1}}\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2^{k}20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{2^{k}20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle+c\sum_{j=1}^{k}2^{-j\left(\frac{sp}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau+s)\right)}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}(x_{0},2^{j}20\rho_{x_{i}})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},

where we denote the two terms on the right-hand side by
T_{3}
and
T_{4}
.
we first estimate
T_{3}
as

\displaystyle T_{3}\leq\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2^{k}20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(2^{k}20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{2^{k}20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq c\lambda,

where we have used (
4.61
) along with (
4.63
) and the fact that

2^{-k\frac{sp}{p-1}}\leq 2^{-k(\alpha+\tau+s)}.

By Hölder’s inequality and (
4.6
), we next estimate
T_{4}
as

\displaystyle T_{4}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{2^{-j\left(\frac{sp}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau+s)\right)}}{\tau^{\frac{1}{p}}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}(x_{0},2^{j}20\rho_{x_{i}})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\leq c\sum_{j=1}^{k}2^{-j\left(\frac{sp}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau+s)\right)}\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p)
.
Combining the estimates of
T_{3}
,
T_{4}
and the fact that

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{\tilde{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}}\leq\lambda,

which follows from Hölder’s inequality and (
4.6
), we have

(4.64)

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tails\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq c\sum_{j=0}^{k}2^{-j\left(\frac{sp}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau+s)\right)}\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p)
. We follow the same lines for proving (
4.64
) with
u(x)
,
s
,
\tilde{p}
,
sp
and
\lambda
there, replaced by
f(x)
,
0
,
\tilde{q}
,
s
and
\delta\lambda
, respectively, in order to obtain that

(4.65)

\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{\tilde{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq c\sum_{j=0}^{k}2^{-j\left(\frac{s}{p-1}-(\alpha+\tau)\right)}\delta\lambda.

Applying (
4.55
) to the last terms in (
4.64
) and (
4.65
), we obtain (
4.58
).

5.
L^{q}
-estimate of
d_{s}u

In the previous section, we have proved Lemma
4.1
which have constructed coverings of upper level sets for
D^{\tau}d_{\alpha+s}u
. We are now going to prove our main theorem
1.2
using a bootstrap argument.
To treat an iterative process, we need to introduce new parameters.
We first note that there is the smallest positive integer
l=l(n,s,p)
such that

(5.1)

n\leq(l+1)p\frac{s}{2}.

Let us start with
p_{0}=p
.
If
l=1
, then define

(5.2)

\gamma_{0}=2q.

Otherwise, for any nonnegative integer
h<l-1
, we inductively define

(5.3)

p_{h+1}=\frac{np_{h}}{n-p_{h}\frac{s}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{h}=\frac{np_{h}}{n-p_{h}s}

with
\gamma_{l-1}=2q
. We note from (
5.1
) and (
5.3
) that

p_{h+1}=\frac{np}{n-(h+1)\frac{sp}{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma_{h}=\frac{np}{n-(h+2)\frac{sp}{2}}\quad\text{for }h<l-1.

Then, we find the smallest nonnegative integer
l_{q}=l_{q}(n,s,p,q)
such that

(5.4)

q<\gamma_{l_{q}}.

Let
u\in W^{s,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(B_{4})\cap L^{p-1}_{sp}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be a weak solution to the localized problem

(5.5)

(-\Delta_{p})_{A}^{s}u=(-\Delta_{p})^{\frac{s}{p}}f\quad\text{in }B_{4}.

Suppose that
f\in L^{p-1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies
d_{0}f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4};\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)
for any

q\in(p,\infty)\quad\text{and}\quad\sigma\in\left(0,\min\left\{\frac{s}{p-1},1-s\right\}\right).

We now divide this section into two subsections depending on the range of
\sigma
below, (
5.6
) and (
5.35
).

5.1
.
Restricted range of fractional differentiability
\sigma
.

We first show the following lemma which will be used to apply a bootstrap argument.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.5
) with

(5.6)

\sigma\in\left(0,\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\right)\min\left\{\frac{s}{p-1},1-s\right\}\right)

and

D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{p_{h}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\mu_{\tau}\right)
, where
h\in\{0,1,\ldots,l_{q}\}
and
\tau=\frac{q}{q-p}\sigma
.
Then there are a small positive constant
\delta=\delta(\mathsf{data})
and a positive constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
independent of
h
such that if
A
is
(\delta,2)
-vanishing in
B_{4}\times B_{4}
only at the diagonal, then

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right),

where the constant
\hat{q}
is defined by

(5.8)

\hat{q}=\begin{cases}p_{h+1}&\quad\text{if }\gamma_{h}\leq q,\\
q&\quad\text{if }\gamma_{h}>q.\end{cases}

Proof.

By Hölder’s inequality and the fact that
\tau=\frac{q}{q-p}\sigma
, we first note

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}=\left(\frac{1}{\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{2}\right)}\iint_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|d_{0}f|^{q}\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}<\infty.

Let
\epsilon\in(0,1)
which will be determined later. We then take
\delta=\delta(n,s,p,\Lambda,\epsilon)
given in Lemma
3.4
. Let
1\leq r_{1}<r_{2}\leq 2
.
We now apply Lemma
4.1
with
\alpha=0,\,\tilde{p}=p_{h}
,
\tilde{q}=p
and
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{h}
to find families of countable disjoint diagonal balls and off-diagonal cubes,
\{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\mathcal{Q}\}_{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}}
, such that

\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\subset\left(\bigcup\limits_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{Q}\right)

for any fixed
\lambda\geq\lambda_{0}
, where
\lambda_{0}
is given in (
4.5
) with
\alpha=0
,
\tilde{p}=p_{h}
and
\tilde{q}=p
. Furthermore, Lemma
4.1
yields (
4.6
), (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) with
\alpha=0,\,\tilde{p}=p_{h}
,
\tilde{q}=p
and
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{h}
.
For
L\geq\lambda_{0}
, we define a function
\phi_{L}(r):[1,2]\to\mathbb{R}
by

\phi_{L}(r)=\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}}\left|D^{\tau}d_{s}u\right|_{L}^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}},

where
\left|D^{\tau}d_{s}u\right|_{L}=\max\{\left|D^{\tau}d_{s}u\right|,L\}
. We now want to show that if
L\geq\lambda_{0}
, then

(5.9)

\displaystyle\phi_{L}(r_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\phi_{L}(r_{2})+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{2n}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{2n}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right)

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
independent of
L
. By (
4.58
) with
\tilde{p}=p_{h},\alpha=0
and
\tilde{q}=p
, and the fact that
\tau<\frac{s}{p-1}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)^{p}\leq(c_{1}\lambda)^{{p}},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{\alpha}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}}}{(20\rho_{x_{i}})^{\alpha+\tau}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)^{p}\leq(\delta c_{1}\lambda)^{p}

for some constant
c_{1}=c_{1}(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
. By the above choice of
\delta=\delta(n,s,p,\Lambda,\epsilon)
, we apply Lemma
3.3
with
\Omega
and
\lambda
there, replaced by
B_{4}
and
c_{1}\lambda
, respectively, to find a weak solution
v
of (
3.26
) satisfying

(5.10)

\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}(v-u)|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\leq(\epsilon c_{1}\lambda)^{p}\quad\text{and}\quad\|D^{\tau}d_{s}v\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i}))}\leq c_{0}c_{1}\lambda

for some constant
c_{0}=c_{0}(\mathsf{data})
, as
\tau
depends only on
p,q
and
\sigma
. From Fubini’s theorem, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{L}^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\hat{q}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{L}(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{M\lambda_{0}}\hat{q}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\int_{M\lambda_{0}}^{L}\hat{q}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\,d\lambda\coloneqq I+J,

where
M>1
will be selected later to control the off-diagonal upper level set of
D^{\tau}d_{s}u
and
L>M\lambda_{0}
. We first observe that

I\leq(M\lambda_{0})^{\hat{q}}\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}).

We next estimate
J
as

(5.11)

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\hat{q}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\hat{q}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\left(\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{Q}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\hat{q}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\left(\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\hat{q}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\left(\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{Q}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2},

where we have used the change of variables for the first equality and the fact that

\displaystyle\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>\lambda\right\}

\displaystyle\subset\left(\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\bigcup\left(\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{Q}\right)

for the second inequality. By taking
M>c_{0}c_{1}
, we find that there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
such that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-v)(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}v(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-v)(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)}\frac{|D^{\tau}d_{s}(u-v)(x,y)|^{p}}{(M\lambda)^{p}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\,d\lambda,

where we have used the second inequality in (
5.10
) for the fourth equality, weak 1-1 estimate for the fifth inequality and the first inequality in (
5.10
) for the sixth inequality. We next note from (
4.6
) with
\alpha=0
,
\tilde{p}=p_{h}
and
\tilde{q}=p
that either

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2c}\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}

or

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{2c}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

holds for some constant
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
. Considering the above two alternatives, we observe that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{4c}{\lambda}\right)^{p_{h}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|>a\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{4c}{\lambda}\right)^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|>b\lambda\right\}}\frac{1}{\delta^{p}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau},

where we have chosen
a=\frac{1}{4c}
and
b=\frac{\delta}{4c}
.
Using (
5.12
), (
5.13
) and the fact that
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right\}_{i}
is the family of disjoint sets contained in
\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}
, we have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i}cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1-p_{h}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|>a\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\sum_{i}cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1-p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|>b\lambda\right\}}\frac{1}{\delta^{p}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{\infty}\lambda^{\hat{q}-p_{h}-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{LM^{-1}}>a\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{\infty}\lambda^{\hat{q}-p-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|>b\lambda\right\}}\frac{1}{\delta^{p}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\,d\lambda

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
. Apply Fubini’s theorem to the last inequality in order to discover that

(5.14)

J_{1}\leq cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|_{LM^{-1}}^{\hat{q}-p_{h}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}+cM^{\hat{q}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\frac{1}{\delta^{\hat{q}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}.
