We now estimate the remaining term
J_{2}
. We first observe that there are constants
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
and
c_{u}=c_{u}(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
such that

(5.15)

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\hat{q}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{Q}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}u(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\hat{q}M^{\hat{q}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|>M\lambda\right\}}\left(\frac{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|}{M\lambda}\right)^{\gamma_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}cM^{\hat{q}-\gamma_{h}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1}\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{Q}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}cM^{\hat{q}-\gamma_{h}}\lambda^{\hat{q}-1-p_{h}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|>c_{u}\lambda\right\}}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\,d\lambda,

where we have used weak 1-1 estimate, (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) with
\alpha=0
,
\tilde{p}=p_{h}
and
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{h}
. Since the value of
\min\{\gamma_{h}-\hat{q}\,;\,h=0,1,\ldots l_{q}\}
is positive and depends only on
n,s,p,q
and
\sigma
, Fubini’s theorem yields that

(5.16)

\displaystyle J_{2}\leq cM^{\hat{q}-\gamma_{h}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau}d_{s}u\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\hat{q}-p_{h}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau}d_{s}u\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

by taking
cM^{\hat{q}-\gamma_{h}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}
and
M>c_{0}c_{1}
.
Since
M
depends only on
\mathsf{data}
, we now choose
\epsilon=\epsilon(\mathsf{data})
sufficiently small so that (
5.14
) becomes

J_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau}d_{s}u\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}+c\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. Combine all the estimates
I,J_{1}
and
J_{2}
to derive that for any
L>M\lambda_{0}
,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\phi_{LM^{-1}}(r_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\phi_{LM^{-1}}(r_{2})+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{2n}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{c}{(r_{2}-r_{1})^{2n}}\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right),

where we have used the fact that

\phi_{LM^{-1}}(r_{1})\leq\phi_{L}(r_{1})

and

\frac{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}})}{\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}})}\leq 2^{2n}.

Thus (
5.9
) follows from (
5.17
). Then, using Lemma
2.8
, we get that if
L>\lambda_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\phi_{L}(1)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right)

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. By passing to the limit
L\to\infty
, we have the desired estimate (
5.7
).
∎

With Lemma
5.1
, we now prove Theorem
1.2
for the restricted range of
\sigma
given in (
5.6
).

Proof of Theorem
1.2
when (
5.6
).
We first take
\delta>0
determined in Lemma
5.1
.
We are now going to show that for any
B_{2r}(x_{0})\Subset\Omega
with
r\in(0,R]
, there is a constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
such that

(5.18)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-\left(u\right)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-\left(f\right)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right),

where

(5.19)

\tau=\frac{q}{q-p}\sigma

and

(5.20)

\hat{q}=\begin{cases}p_{1}&\quad\text{if }\gamma_{0}\leq q,\\
q&\quad\text{if }\gamma_{0}>q.\end{cases}

Let
y_{0}\in B_{2r}(x_{0})
. We define

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(x)=\left(\frac{r}{8}\right)^{-(\tau+s)}u\left(\frac{r}{8}x+y_{0}\right),\quad\tilde{f}(x)=\left(\frac{r}{8}\right)^{-\tau}f\left(\frac{r}{8}x+y_{0}\right),\quad\tilde{A}(x,y)=A\left(\frac{r}{8}x+y_{0},\frac{r}{8}y+y_{0}\right)

for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then
\tilde{u}\in W^{s,p}(B_{4})\cap L^{p-1}_{sp}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is a weak solution to

(-\Delta)_{p,\tilde{A}}^{s}\tilde{u}=(-\Delta_{p})^{\frac{s}{p}}\tilde{f}\quad\text{in }B_{4},

where
\tilde{A}
is diagonal
(\delta,2)
-vanishing in
B_{4}\times B_{4}
. In addition, we observe that

d_{0}\tilde{f}\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4};\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\mu_{\tau}\right).

Therefore, we apply Lemma
5.1
with
u=\tilde{u}
,
f=\tilde{f}
,
A=\tilde{A}
and
h=0
to see that

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-\left(\tilde{u}\right)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau}d_{0}\tilde{f}|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{\tilde{f}-\left(\tilde{f}\right)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\tau}};B_{2}\right)\right)

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. Using the change of variables, we then observe that

(5.21)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{4}\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{4}\right)^{\tau}};B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})\right)\right).

Applying (
2.10
) with
\rho=\frac{r}{4}
,
R=2r
,
\alpha=0
and
t=s
and then using (
5.19
), we find that

(5.22)

\displaystyle\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{4}\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. Likewise, (
2.10
) with
u(x)=f(x)

\rho=\frac{r}{4}
,

,

,
\alpha=0
and

, and (

) yield that

\displaystyle\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{4}\right)^{\tau}};B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)+c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}},

where we have used Hölder’s inequality for the last inequality. By inserting (
5.22
) and (
5.23
) into (
5.21
) and using the fact that
\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(y_{0})\subset\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})
, we have

(5.24)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(y_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}\tilde{f}|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right).

On the other hand, using Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that
\hat{q}\leq\gamma_{0}
by (

), (

) and (
5.20
), and then applying Lemma
4.2
with
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{0}
,
\alpha=0
and
\tilde{p}=p
, we find that there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
such that for any
\mathcal{Q}\equiv Q_{\frac{r}{8\sqrt{n}}}(z_{1})\times Q_{\frac{r}{8\sqrt{n}}}(z_{2})\Subset\Omega\times\Omega
satisfying (

),

(5.25)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\gamma_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\tau}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\right].

Let us assume that
z_{1},z_{2}\in B_{r}(x_{0})
. From (
4.32
) together with the observation that

\frac{r}{8\sqrt{n}}=d(\mathcal{Q})<|x-y|<r\quad\text{for any }(x,y)\in\mathcal{Q},

we have

(5.26)

\frac{r^{n+p\tau}}{c}\leq\mu_{\tau}(\mathcal{Q})\leq cr^{n+p\tau}

for some constant
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
.
Using (
5.19
), (
5.26
) and the fact that
\mathcal{Q},P^{1}\mathcal{Q},P^{2}\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})
, we further estimate the both sides of (
5.25
) to see that

(5.27)

\left(\frac{1}{r^{n+p\tau}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. Since we can cover
\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})
with finitely many diagonal balls
\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}\left(y_{i}\right)
and off-diagonal cubes
\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{8\sqrt{n}}}\left(z_{1,i},z_{2,i}\right)
satisfying (
4.30
) for some
y_{i},\,z_{1,i},\,z_{2,i}\in B_{r}(x_{0})
,
the standard covering argument along with (
5.19
), (
5.24
) and (
5.27
) leads to (
5.18
). Since we have arbitrarily chosen
x_{0},z_{1},z_{2}\in\Omega
and
r\in(0,R]
satisfying
B_{2r}(x_{0})\Subset\Omega
and
\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{8\sqrt{n}}}\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)\Subset\Omega\times\Omega
with (
4.30
), it follows from (
5.18
) and (
5.25
) that
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{\hat{q}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega\,;\mu_{\tau}\right)
.
If
l_{q}=0
, where
l_{q}
is given in (
5.4
), then
\hat{q}=q
. Let us assume that
l_{q}>0
. Then the fact that
\hat{q}=p_{1}
yields
D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}\in L^{p_{1}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\mu_{\tau}\right)
. Thus, we apply Lemma
5.1
with
u=\tilde{u}
,
f=\tilde{f}
,
A=\tilde{A}
and
h=1
, and follow the same arguments as in (
5.21
) to find that

(5.28)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{4}\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{4}\right)^{\tau}};B_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})\right)\right),

where

(5.29)

\hat{q}_{1}=\begin{cases}p_{2}&\quad\text{if }\gamma_{1}\leq q,\\
q&\quad\text{if }\gamma_{1}>q.\end{cases}

Inserting (
2.10
) with
\rho=\frac{r}{4}
,
R=2r
,
\alpha=0
and
t=s
into (
5.18
) with
x_{0}
and

there, replaced by
y_{0}
and
\frac{r}{4}
, respectively, and then using the fact that
\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2}}(y_{0})\subset\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})
, we have

(5.30)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{4}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-\left(u\right)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-\left(f\right)_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(y_{0})}}{\left(\frac{r}{2}\right)^{\tau}};B_{\frac{r}{2}}(y_{0})\right)\right),

where we have used Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that
p_{1}\leq\hat{q}_{1}
for the third term in the right-hand side of (
5.30
). We now combine (
5.28
) and (
5.30
) to obtain that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(y_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(y_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right).

For the fourth terms in the right-hand side of (
5.28
) and (
5.30
), we have used (
2.10
) with
u(x)=f(x)

\rho=\frac{r}{2}
or
\frac{r}{4}
,
R=2r
,
s=\frac{s}{p}
,
\alpha=0
and
t=0
, and Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that
p\leq\hat{q}_{1}
.
Furthermore, as in (
5.25
), Lemma
4.2
with
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{1}
,
\alpha=0
and
\tilde{p}=p_{1}
yields

(5.32)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c\left[\sum_{d=1}^{2}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}\right],

provided that
\mathcal{Q}=\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{8\sqrt{n}}}\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)\Subset\Omega\times\Omega
satisfying (
4.30
). Let
z_{1},z_{2}\in B_{r}(x_{0})
. Since
P^{d}\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(z_{d})
, Hölder’s inequality and the same computations as in (
5.31
) with
y_{0}=z_{d}
give that

(5.33)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{P^{d}\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{8}}(z_{d})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{\frac{r}{2}}(y_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right).

In light of (
5.26
), (
5.33
) and the fact that
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})
, we further estimate the both sides of (
5.32
) to see

(5.34)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{r^{n+p\tau}}\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau}d_{0}f|^{\hat{q}_{1}}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}_{1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{\left(2r\right)^{\tau}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right).

As in the case when
l_{q}=0
, the standard covering argument together with (
5.19
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
) and (
5.34
) yields (
5.18
) with
\hat{q}=\hat{q}_{1}
and
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{\tilde{q}_{1}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega\,;\,\mu_{\tau}\right)
. By iterating the above procedure
l_{q}-1
times, we obtain (
5.18
) with
\hat{q}=q
and
D^{\tau}d_{s}u\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega\times\Omega\,;\,\mu_{\tau}\right)
. Thus, the desired estimate (
1.8
) follows by recalling the definition (
5.19
) and using a few algebraic computations. It completes the proof. ∎

5.2
.
Improved range of fractional differentiability
\sigma

We first prove a comparison estimate of the fractional gradient
d_{s+\alpha}u
for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
To this end, we introduce new parameters.
We first assume that

(5.35)

\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\right)\min\left\{\frac{s}{p-1},1-s\right\}\leq\sigma<\min\left\{\frac{s}{p-1},1-s\right\}.

We then choose
\beta\in(0,1)
such that

(5.36)

\sigma=\beta\min\left\{\frac{s}{p-1},1-s\right\}.

For each
m=0,1,\ldots,
we now take

(5.37)

\tilde{\tau}_{0}=\frac{\beta+1}{2}\min\left\{\frac{s}{p-1},1-s\right\},\quad q_{0}=\begin{cases}\min\{p_{1},q\}&\quad\text{if }sp\leq n\\
q&\quad\text{if }sp>n\end{cases}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\tau}_{m+1}=\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\tilde{\tau}_{m}

and

(5.38)

\tilde{\alpha}_{m}=\tilde{\tau}_{0}-\tilde{\tau}_{m}

to see that

(5.39)

\tilde{\alpha}_{m}+\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\right)\tilde{\tau}_{m}=\tilde{\tau_{0}}-\frac{p}{q}\tilde{\tau}_{m}=\tilde{\tau}_{0}\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\left(\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\right)^{m}\right),

where
p_{1}
is given in (
5.3
).
From (
5.35
), (
5.36
), (
5.37
) and the fact that

\tilde{\tau}_{0}\left(1-\left(\frac{p}{q}\right)\right)<\sigma\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\tau}_{0}>\sigma,

there is a positive integer
n_{\sigma}=n_{\sigma}(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
such that

\tilde{\tau}_{0}\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\left(\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\right)^{n_{\sigma}}\right)\geq\sigma\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{\tau}_{0}\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\left(\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\right)^{n_{\sigma}-1}\right)<\sigma.

We now select
\tau_{0}\in(0,\tilde{\tau}_{0}]
such that

(5.40)

\tau_{0}\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\left(\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\right)^{n_{\sigma}}\right)=\sigma\quad\text{and}\quad\tau_{0}\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\left(\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\right)^{n_{\sigma}-1}\right)<\sigma.

Similarly, we take

(5.41)

\tau_{m+1}=\frac{q_{0}+p}{2q_{0}}\tau_{m}\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_{m}=\tau_{0}-\tau_{m}

to observe that

(5.42)

\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\right)\tau_{n_{\sigma}}=\sigma\quad\text{and}\quad\alpha_{i}+\left(1-\frac{p}{q}\right)\tau_{i}<\sigma\quad(i=0,1,\ldots,n_{\sigma}-1),

where we have used (
5.38
), (
5.39
), (
5.40
) and (
5.41
).
In this setting, we define

\tilde{p}_{m}=p+\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{1}{2^{i+2}}\frac{q_{0}-p}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{q}_{m}=q_{0}\left(\frac{3p+q_{0}}{2(q_{0}+p)}+\sum_{i=0}^{m}\frac{1}{2^{i+2}}\frac{q_{0}-p}{2(q_{0}+p)}\right)

to find that

(5.43)

p<\tilde{p}_{m}<\frac{3p+q_{0}}{4}<q_{0}\frac{3p+q_{0}}{2(q_{0}+p)}<\tilde{q}_{m}<q_{0}.

In light of (
5.43
) and (
5.41
), we find

(5.44)

\alpha_{m}+\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{p}_{m}}\right)\tau_{m}<\alpha_{m-1}+\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{m}}\right)\tau_{m-1}.

Fix a nonnegative integer
m\in[0,n_{\sigma}]
. Throughout the proof of the next two lemmas, we assume that any weak solution

u\in W^{s,p}_{\mathrm{loc}}(B_{4})\cap L^{p-1}_{sp}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to the localized problem

(5.45)

(-\Delta_{p})_{A}^{s}u=(-\Delta_{p})^{\frac{s}{p}}f\quad\text{in }B_{4},

where
f\in L^{p-1}_{s}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
with
d_{0}f\in L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4};\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n+\sigma q}}\right)
satisfies
D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\,\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)
with the estimates

(5.46)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leq d_{m}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+d_{m}\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}f|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

for some constant
d_{m}>0
, provided that
B_{4r}(x_{0})\Subset B_{4}
. We remark that applying Lemma
2.4
with
p=\tilde{q}_{m}
,
s_{1}=\alpha_{m}+\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{m}}\right)\tau_{m}
and
s_{2}=\sigma
, we discover that

\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}f|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m}}}\leq c[f]_{W^{\alpha_{m}+\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{m}}\right)\tau_{m},\tilde{q}_{m}}(B_{2r}(x_{0}))}\leq c[f]_{W^{\sigma,q}(B_{2r}(x_{0}))}<\infty,

which implies that the right-hand side of (
5.46
) is well-defined. We now improve the comparison lemma
3.4
using an interpolation argument.

Lemma 5.2
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there is a constant
\delta=\delta(\mathsf{data},\epsilon,d_{m})
such that for any weak solution
u
to (
5.45
) with

B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\Subset B_{4}

and

(5.47)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(4r)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)^{p}\leq\lambda^{p},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}f|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}}{(4r)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}};B_{20\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)^{\tilde{q}_{m}}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{10\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{10\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}\lambda|A(x,y)-(A)_{B_{10\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\times B_{10\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|\,dx\,dy\right)^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\leq(\delta\lambda)^{\tilde{q}_{m}},

there exists a weak solution
v\in W^{s,p}(B_{10\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i}))\cap L^{p-1}_{sp}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to

(5.48)

(-\Delta)_{p,A_{10\rho_{x_{i}},x_{i}}}^{s}v=0\quad\text{in }B_{10\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})

such that

(5.49)

\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}(u-v)|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\leq(\epsilon\lambda)^{p}\quad\text{and}\quad\|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}v\|_{L^{\infty}\left(\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)}\leq c_{2}\lambda

for some constant
c_{2}=c_{2}(\mathsf{data})
.

Proof.

Let us define for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\tilde{u}(x)=\frac{u(5\rho_{x_{i}}x+x_{i})}{\left(5\rho_{x_{i}}\right)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}\lambda},\quad\tilde{f}(x)=\frac{f(5\rho_{x_{i}}x+x_{i})}{\left(5\rho_{x_{i}}\right)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}\lambda}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{A}(x,y)=A(5\rho_{x_{i}}x+x_{i},5\rho_{x_{i}}y+x_{i})

to see that

(-\Delta)_{p,\tilde{A}}^{s}\tilde{u}=(-\Delta_{p})^{\frac{s}{p}}\tilde{f}\quad\text{in }B_{4}

and

(5.50)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{4}\right)^{p}\leq 1,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\Tails\left(\frac{\tilde{f}-(\tilde{f})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}};B_{4}\right)^{\tilde{q}_{m}}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}|\tilde{A}(x,y)-(\tilde{A})_{B_{2}\times B_{2}}|\,dx\,dy\right)^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\leq\delta^{\tilde{q}_{m}},

which follows from (
5.47
). We first observe that there is a constant
c=c(n,s,p,q,\sigma)
such that

(5.51)

\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{0}\tilde{f}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\leq\frac{1}{\tau_{m}^{1-\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{m}}}}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{0}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{m}}}\leq c,

where we have used Hölder’s inequality. Combining the estimates (
5.50
) and (
5.51
), and then using the fact

1\leq\frac{8^{\alpha_{m}}}{|x-y|^{\alpha_{m}}}\leq\frac{c}{|x-y|^{\alpha_{m}}}\quad\text{for }x,y\in B_{4}

and

(5.52)

\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\leq\frac{c}{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\leq c,

we find that there exists a constant

depending only on

and

such that

(5.53)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\tau_{m}+s}};B_{4}\right)^{p}\leq c^{p},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{0}\tilde{f}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\Tails\left(\frac{\tilde{f}-(\tilde{f})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\tau_{m}}};B_{4}\right)^{p}

\displaystyle+\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}c\left|\tilde{A}(x,y)-(\tilde{A})_{B_{2}\times B_{2}}\right|\,dx\,dy\right)^{p}\leq(\delta c)^{p}.

By following the same lines as in the proof of (
3.20
) and (
3.24
), we observe that there is a weak solution
\tilde{v}\in W^{s,p}(B_{2})\cap L^{p-1}_{sp}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
to

(5.54)

such that

(5.55)

\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{s}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{v})|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\leq c\left(\delta^{\nu}+\delta^{p}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\Tailp(\tilde{v}-(\tilde{v})_{B_{2}};B_{2})\leq c

for some constants

and

. In light of Lemma

along with the fact that

, (

\displaystyle\|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{1})}

\displaystyle=\|\tilde{v}\|_{C^{s+\tau_{0}}(B_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2}}|\tilde{v}-(\tilde{v})_{B_{2}}|^{p}\,dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c\Tailp(\tilde{v}-(\tilde{v})_{B_{2}};B_{2})

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{s}\tilde{v}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{s}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{v})|^{p}+|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c

\displaystyle\leq c_{2}.

We next note from (

) that

\displaystyle\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{2}\right)\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+c\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{4}\right)

and

where we have used Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that
p\leq\tilde{q}_{m}
for the first term in the right-hand side of the above inequality. Combining the above two inequalities, the scaled version of (

) with

,

and

, and (

(5.57)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{\tilde{p}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p_{m}}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{4}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+c\left[\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{4}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{\tilde{f}-(\tilde{f})_{B_{4}}}{4^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}};B_{4}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq c

for some constant

. Applying Lemma

with

,

\displaystyle{}_{W^{s+\alpha_{m}+\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{p}_{m}}\right)\tau_{m},p}(B_{1})}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{v})|^{\tilde{p}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{\tilde{p}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m}}}

\displaystyle+c\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{v}|^{\tilde{p}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leq c.

with

,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{v})|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

We define

∎

By following the same strategy as for the proof of Lemma

, we now obtain (

) with

.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (

), and let

.

-vanishing in

) with

Proof.

Let
B_{4r}(x_{0})\Subset B_{4}
for some

.

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(x)=\frac{u\left(rx+x_{0}\right)}{r^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}},\quad\tilde{f}(x)=\frac{f\left(rx+x_{0}\right)}{r^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\tilde{A}(x,y)=A\left(rx+x_{0},ry+x_{0}\right)

(\delta,2)
-vanishing in
B_{4}\times B_{4}
only at the diagonal. We first want to show that

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{\tilde{u}-(\tilde{u})_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{2}\right)\right]

for some constant

.
We fix

, and then take

given in Lemma
5.2
. Let

with

,

to gain families of countable disjoint diagonal balls and off-diagonal cubes,

for some constant

. We then apply Lemma
5.2
with

there, replaced by

, in order to obtain a weak solution
\tilde{v}
of (

) satisfying

(5.62)

\frac{1}{\tau_{m}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}(\tilde{u}-\tilde{v})|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\leq(\epsilon c_{1}\lambda)^{p}\quad\text{and}\quad\|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i}))}\leq c_{2}c_{1}\lambda,

where the constant
c_{2}
is given in Lemma
5.2
.
We first notice from Fubini’s theorem that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}}\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|_{L}^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\tilde{q}_{m+1}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m}}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|_{L}(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{M\lambda_{0}}\tilde{q}_{m+1}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m}}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\int_{M\lambda_{0}}^{L}\tilde{q}_{m+1}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m}}\left\{(x,y)\in\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}\,;\,|D^{\tau}d_{s}\tilde{u}|(x,y)>\lambda\right\}\,d\lambda\coloneqq I+J,

where
M>1
will be selected later and
L>M\lambda_{0}
. We now estimate
I
as

I\leq(M\lambda_{0})^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\mu_{\tau_{m}}(\mathcal{B}_{r_{1}}).

Proceeding as for (
5.11
) leads to

\displaystyle J

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\tilde{q}_{m+1}M^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m}}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\left(\bigcup\limits_{i}\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\tilde{q}_{m+1}M^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m}}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\left(\bigcup\limits_{\mathcal{Q}\in\tilde{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{Q}\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\eqqcolon J_{1}+J_{2}.

By following the same arguments as in (
5.12
) along with (
5.62
), we have that there is a constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
such that

(5.63)

\displaystyle\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}M^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\left(\left\{(x,y)\in\left(\mathcal{B}_{5\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\,;\,|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}(x,y)|>M\lambda\right\}\right)\,d\lambda

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{LM^{-1}}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-1}\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}\left(x_{i}\right)\right)\,d\lambda,

where we have taken
M>c_{1}c_{2}
. We next note from (
4.6
) with
\tilde{p}=p
,
\tilde{q}=\tilde{q}_{m}
,
\alpha=\alpha_{m}
and
\tau=\tau_{m}
that either

(5.64)

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{m}^{\frac{1}{p}}}{c_{d}}\frac{\lambda}{2}\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

or

(5.65)

\displaystyle\frac{\tau_{m}^{\frac{1}{p}}}{c_{d}}\frac{\lambda}{2}\leq\frac{1}{\delta}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m}}}

holds. After a few algebraic manipulations along with (
5.52
), (
5.64
) and (
5.65
), we find that

\mu_{\tau}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)\leq\left(\frac{c}{\lambda}\right)^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+\left(\frac{c}{\delta\lambda}\right)^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. We then observe that

(5.66)

\displaystyle\mu_{\tau_{m}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{2c}{\lambda}\right)^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\cap\left\{|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|>a\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{2c}{\delta\lambda}\right)^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\cap\left\{|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|>b\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}},

where
a=\frac{1}{4c}
and

.
Combine (
5.63
) and (
5.66
) together with the fact that
\left\{\mathcal{B}_{\rho_{x_{i}}}(x_{i})\right\}
is a collection of disjoint sets contained in
\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}
to see that

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{\infty}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|_{LM^{-1}}>a\lambda\right\}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\,d\lambda

\displaystyle+cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{\infty}\lambda^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-\tilde{q}_{m}-1}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}\cap\left\{|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|>b\lambda\right\}}\frac{1}{\delta^{\tilde{q}_{m}}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\,d\lambda

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. We then have

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

\displaystyle+cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-\tilde{q}_{m}}\epsilon^{p}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\frac{1}{\delta^{\tilde{q}_{m}+1}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}},

where we have used Fubini’s theorem. Since
\gamma_{0}>\tilde{q}_{m+1}
, similar arguments performed on the estimates of (
5.15
) and (
5.16
) along with (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) with
\alpha=\alpha_{m},\,\tilde{p}=p
,
\tilde{\gamma}=\gamma_{0}
and
\tau=\tau_{m}
yield that

\displaystyle J_{2}

\displaystyle\leq cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-\gamma_{0}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

by taking

sufficiently large such that
cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-\gamma_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}
and
M>c_{1}c_{2}
depending only on
\mathsf{data}
. We now choose a sufficiently small
\epsilon=\epsilon(\mathsf{data})
such that
cM^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}-p}\epsilon^{p}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}
to see that

J_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2^{2n+2q}}\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}\left|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}\tilde{u}\right|_{LM^{-1}}^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}+c\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r_{2}}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}\tilde{f}|^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data},d_{m})
, as
\delta
depends only on
\mathsf{data}
and
d_{m}
.
In light of the estimates
I
,
J_{1}
and
J_{2}
, the same arguments as for (
5.17
) yield (
5.61
). Then (
5.60
) follows by using the technical lemma
2.8
and the limit procedure as in the last part for the proof of Lemma
5.1
. Using the change of variables and (
5.60
), we find that

(5.67)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}f|^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\alpha_{m}+\tau_{m}}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data},d_{m})
. By (
5.44
), we now apply Lemma
2.4
with
p=\tilde{p}_{m+1}
,
q=\tilde{q}_{m+1}
,
s_{1}=\alpha_{m+1}+\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}\right)\tau_{m+1}
and

, and use (
5.41
) and (
5.52
) to see that

(5.68)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}}

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. Using (
5.68
), the fact that

(5.69)

1\leq\left(\frac{4r}{|x-y|}\right)^{\alpha_{m+1}-\alpha_{m}}\quad\text{for }x,y\in\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})

and (
5.41
), we further estimate the left-hand side and the third term in the right-hand side in (

) to find that

(5.70)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\alpha_{m+1}+\tau_{m+1}+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}}f|^{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{m+1}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\alpha_{m+1}+\tau_{m+1}}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data},d_{m})
provided that
\mathcal{B}_{4r}(x_{0})\Subset\mathcal{B}_{4}
. Using Hölder’s inequality and (
5.70
), we have

(5.71)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{m+1}}}<\infty.

On the other hand, for any cube
\mathcal{Q}\equiv\mathcal{Q}_{\frac{r}{\sqrt{n}}}\left(z_{1},z_{2}\right)
where
z_{1},z_{2}\in B_{4}
satisfying (
4.30
), we find that

(5.72)

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\leq c(\mathsf{data},r)\left(\int_{\mathcal{Q}}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}<\infty,

where we have used (
5.41
) and the fact that
\frac{1}{8}\leq\frac{1}{|x-y|}\leq\frac{\sqrt{n}}{r}
for any
(x,y)\in\mathcal{Q}
. Thus, the standard covering argument along with (
5.71
) and (
5.72
) yields
D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\,\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)
. Lastly, using (
5.69
), we estimate the first term in the right-hand side of (
5.70
) to get (
5.46
) with
m
there, replaced by
m+1
, where
d_{m+1}=d_{m+1}(\mathsf{data},d_{m})
.
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
5.5
) with (
5.35
). Then there is a small constant
\delta=\delta(\mathsf{data})
such that if
A
is
(\delta,2)
-vanishing in
B_{4}\times B_{4}
only at the diagonal, then we have that
(
5.46
) with
m=n_{\sigma}
, where
d_{n_{\sigma}}=d_{n_{\sigma}}(\mathsf{data})
, and

D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+s}u\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\,\mu_{\tau_{n\sigma}}\right)
with the estimate

(5.73)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2^{n_{\sigma}-1}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\tau_{0}}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
whenever
B_{2r}(x_{0})\Subset B_{4}
.

Proof.

From Lemma
2.4
,
f\in W^{\sigma,q}_{\mathrm{loc}}(B_{4})
implies
f\in W^{\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{0}}\right)\tau_{0},\tilde{q}_{0}}_{\mathrm{loc}}(B_{4})
, which is equivalent to

d_{0}f\in L^{\tilde{q}_{0}}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4};\frac{\,dx\,dy}{|x-y|^{n+\left(\tilde{q}_{0}-p\right)\tau_{0}}}\right).

We now apply Theorem
1.2
with
\sigma=\left(1-\frac{p}{\tilde{q}_{0}}\right)\tau_{0}
to find a small constant
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\mathsf{data})\in(0,1)
. Thus if
\delta<\delta_{0}
, then we have

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{q}_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{0}}}

\displaystyle\leq d_{0}\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+d_{0}\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{0}f|^{\tilde{q}_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{0}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2r}(x_{0})}}{(2r)^{\tau_{0}}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)\right]

for some constant
d_{0}=d_{0}(\mathsf{data})
, provided that
B_{2r}(x_{0})\Subset B_{4}
. Since Hölder’s inequality yields that

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{p}_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{p}_{0}}}\leq\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{\tilde{q}_{0}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\tilde{q}_{0}}},

the above two inequalities give that (
5.46
) with
m=0
, where
d_{0}=d_{0}(\mathsf{data})
.
By applying Lemma
5.3
with
m=0
, we have that (
5.46
) with
m=1
, where
d_{1}=d_{1}(\mathsf{data})
whenever
\delta<\min\{\delta_{0},\delta_{1}\}
, where the constant
\delta_{1}
is determined by Lemma
5.3
with
m=0
. By iterating this procedure
n_{\sigma}-1
times, we conclude that if
\delta<\min\limits_{h=0,1,\ldots,n_{\sigma}}\delta_{h}
, where
\delta_{h}
is the constant given in Lemma
5.3
with
m=h-1
for
h\geq 1
, then
D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+s}u\in L^{p}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\mathcal{B}_{4}\,;\,\mu_{\tau_{n\sigma}}\right)
and (
5.46
) with
m=n_{\sigma}
is true, where
d_{n_{\sigma}}=d_{n_{\sigma}}(\mathsf{data})
.
We are now in the position to prove (
5.73
).
Combining the first inequality in (
5.71
) and (
5.70
) with
r
there, replaced by
\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}
, respectively, and then applying Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that
\tilde{q}_{m+1}<q
to the third term in the right-hand side of (
5.70
), we get that

(5.74)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2^{m}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m}+\alpha_{m}}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}{(\frac{r}{2^{m-1}})^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}}{(\frac{r}{2^{m-1}})^{\tau_{0}}};B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})\right)\right].

Combine the above estimates (
5.74
) for
m=0,1,\ldots,n_{\sigma}-1
to see that

(5.75)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2^{n_{\sigma}-1}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{0}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}+\sum_{m=0}^{n_{\sigma}-1}\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{2r}(x_{0})}{(\frac{r}{2^{m-1}})^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})\right)\right]

\displaystyle+c\sum_{m=0}^{n_{\sigma}-1}\left[\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{m+1}}d_{\alpha_{m+1}}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m+1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}}{(\frac{r}{2^{m-1}})^{\tau_{0}}};B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})\right)\right],

where
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. We next note from (
5.41
) and (
5.52
) that for any
\mathcal{B}\Subset\mathcal{B}_{4}
,

(5.76)

\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}}|D^{\tau_{m-1}}d_{\alpha_{m-1}}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m-1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}}|D^{\tau_{m}}d_{\alpha_{m}}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau_{m}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}},

where
c=c(\mathsf{data})
. In addition, applying (
2.9
) with
\tau=\tau_{0}
,
t=s
,
\alpha=0
,
\rho=\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}
and

leads to

(5.77)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{n_{\sigma}-1}\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}{(\frac{r}{2^{m-1}})^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{s}u|^{p}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle+c\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{2r}(x_{0})}{(2r)^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right)

for some constant
c=c(\mathsf{data})
, as
n_{\sigma}
and
\tau_{0}
depend only on
\mathsf{data}
. Similarly, applying (
2.9
) with
u(x)
,
s
,
\tau
,

,
\alpha
,
\rho
and
i
there, replaced by

,
\frac{s}{p}
,
\tau_{0}
,
0
,
0
,
\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}
and
m
, respectively, and then using Hölder’s inequality along with the fact that

, we find

(5.78)

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{n_{\sigma}-1}\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})}{(\frac{r}{2^{m-1}})^{\tau_{0}+s}};B_{\frac{r}{2^{m-1}}}(x_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2r}(x_{0})}|D^{\tau_{0}}d_{0}f|^{q}\,d\mu_{\tau_{0}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle+c\Tailp\left(\frac{f-(f)_{2r}(x_{0})}{(2r)^{\tau_{0}}};B_{2r}(x_{0})\right).

We combine the estimates (

), (
5.76
), (
5.77
) and (
5.78
) to show that (
5.73
).
∎

We now give a similar result corresponding to Lemma

.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let

be a weak solution to (

) with (

only at the diagonal, then we have

(5.79)

\displaystyle\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{1}}|D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+s}u|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}

\displaystyle\leq c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+s}u|^{p_{h}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{p_{h}}}+\Tailp\left(\frac{u-(u)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+\tau_{n_{\sigma}}+s}};B_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+c\left(\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{\mathcal{B}_{2}}|D^{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}d_{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}}f|^{\hat{q}}\,d\mu_{\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\hat{q}}}+\Tails\left(\frac{f-(f)_{B_{2}}}{2^{\alpha_{n_{\sigma}}+\tau_{n_{\sigma}}}};B_{2}\right)\right),

Proof.

for some constant

We now give the complete proof of our main theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.
Since we proved Theorem
1.2
when (
5.6
) in Section

, we may assume (
5.35
).
Take

, where the constant
